I don't really use cover-ups or anything like that. But... I guess I do check a lot. I don't know. I catch myself staring in the bathroom mirror, or the one in the truck. I'll keep adjusting my hat, trying to pull it down further... just trying to get it right. And I end up looking at my hands, thinking about how they look compared to the younger guys who help out during harvest. I don't know... I guess I end up wasting a fair bit of time doing that when I should be out working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about how your skin looks would still bother you even if someone reassured you that it looks normal or nothing out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well... my wife thought I should come talk to someone. She says I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors all his life, but... I don't know. I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. \n\nThe cracks, the sun spots, the sagging... it's just hard to ignore. Whenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or the county people, I just... I know they're staring right at it. So... I guess that's what brings me in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself spending a lot of time thinking about these skin changes, or do you feel that they interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I suppose I do think about it a fair bit. And... well, yeah, it does get in the way sometimes. \n\nI've actually missed a couple of calls with the bankers lately. I just... I put off some farm decisions because I couldn't get myself looking... acceptable enough to go sit down with people. And my wife... well, I stopped agreeing to let her take photos of me to promote the retreat and the craft sales we do. I just... I don't want to be seen like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the changes in your skin look as bad to others as they do to you, or do you think others might not notice them as much as you do?\n\nPatient: I... I couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. \n\nMy wife claims they don't notice it, but... I don't know. It's hard to imagine they don't see it when it's right there on my face. I just figure they have to be looking at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your skin changes are abnormal or different from what someone your age and lifestyle would normally have?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I mean, I know guys my age who farm, and they have lines and such. But... mine just seems worse. I don't really know what's normal anymore. I just know what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your skin changes make you look physically deformed or unrecognizable to yourself, or do you think it's more about how others might perceive it?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I don't know if \"deformed\" is the right word. I just... I don't like how it looks. And I guess I worry about what other people think when they see me. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people avoid looking at you, or act differently around you, because of how your skin looks?\n\nPatient: I couldn't say for sure. I mostly just keep to myself... out on the farm, anyway. So, I haven't really noticed anyone doing that.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly about a specific part of your skin, like a particular area on your face or hands, or does it feel more spread out and bothersome across many areas?\n\nPatient: Well... it's mostly my face and hands. I just... I can't get past how rough and worn they look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... it just bothers me. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't know. When I have to go into town, see the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself spending a lot of time each day thinking about or trying to fix or hide these skin concerns, such as checking your appearance, using cover-ups, or avoiding mirrors?\n\nPatient: I don't really use cover-ups or anything like that. But... I guess I do check a lot. Especially before I have to go into town for a loan meeting, or if my wife drags me to one of those craft fairs to help with her table. I just... I catch myself staring in the bathroom mirror, or the one in the truck. I'll keep adjusting my hat, trying to pull it down further... just trying to get it right. And I end up looking at my hands, thinking about how they look compared to the younger guys who help out during harvest. I don't know... I guess I end up wasting a fair bit of time doing that when I should be out working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about how your skin looks would still bother you even if someone reassured you that it looks normal or nothing out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I was saying, my wife already tells me it's just normal for a guy my age who works outside. But... I guess it doesn't really change how I feel about it. It just... still bothers me, no matter what she says.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin changes you’re concerned about are something that most people probably wouldn’t even notice unless you pointed it out to them?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. I just feel like when I'm in town, every banker or county person is staring right at it, even if she thinks they aren't.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly focused on how your skin looks, or does it also extend to other parts of your appearance, like your weight, hair, or overall body shape?\n\nPatient: No, it's... it's just my skin. I mean, my hair's thinning, but I'm 62, so that's... that's just how it goes. It's really just my face and my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or self-conscious about your appearance in social situations, especially when people you don’t know might see you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. I'd rather not be seen.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I'm not really sure how to answer that. I don't know if \"deformed\" is the right word. I just... I don't like how it looks. And I guess I worry about what other people think when they see me. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people avoid looking at you, or act differently around you, because of how your skin looks?\n\nPatient: I couldn't say for sure. I mostly just keep to myself... out on the farm, anyway. So, I haven't really noticed anyone doing that.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly about a specific part of your skin, like a particular area on your face or hands, or does it feel more spread out and bothersome across many areas?\n\nPatient: Well... it's mostly my face and hands. I just... I can't get past how rough and worn they look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... it just bothers me. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't know. When I have to go into town, see the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself spending a lot of time each day thinking about or trying to fix or hide these skin concerns, such as checking your appearance, using cover-ups, or avoiding mirrors?\n\nPatient: I don't really use cover-ups or anything like that. But... I guess I do check a lot. Especially before I have to go into town for a loan meeting, or if my wife drags me to one of those craft fairs to help with her table. I just... I catch myself staring in the bathroom mirror, or the one in the truck. I'll keep adjusting my hat, trying to pull it down further... just trying to get it right. And I end up looking at my hands, thinking about how they look compared to the younger guys who help out during harvest. I don't know... I guess I end up wasting a fair bit of time doing that when I should be out working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about how your skin looks would still bother you even if someone reassured you that it looks normal or nothing out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I was saying, my wife already tells me it's just normal for a guy my age who works outside. But... I guess it doesn't really change how I feel about it. It just... still bothers me, no matter what she says.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin changes you’re concerned about are something that most people probably wouldn’t even notice unless you pointed it out to them?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. My wife says they wouldn't. She says I just look like a guy who's worked outdoors his whole life. But... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I just feel like when I'm in town, every banker or county person is staring right at it, even if she thinks they aren't.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly focused on how your skin looks, or does it also extend to other parts of your appearance, like your weight, hair, or overall body shape?\n\nPatient: No, it's... it's just my skin. I mean, my hair's thinning, but I'm 62, so that's... that's just how it goes. It's really just my face and my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or self-conscious about your appearance in social situations, especially when people you don’t know might see you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It... it gets in the way of things sometimes. Like, I've actually missed a few meetings with the bankers, or... just put off some farm decisions entirely. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to go sit down with people. And... well, we have this retreat and do some craft sales, and I've stopped agreeing to let them take photos of me to promote it. I just... I'd rather not be seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the skin issues you're concerned about are something that other people would clearly see and notice, or do you think most people might not even pick up on them unless you mentioned it?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said a minute ago... my wife doesn't think they notice. She tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors. But... I don't know. I just can't get past it. My face and hands just look so rough and worn. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I feel like they clearly see it. It just feels like every banker or county person is staring right at it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. \n\nMy wife claims they don't notice it, but... I don't know. It's hard to imagine they don't see it when it's right there on my face. I just figure they have to be looking at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your skin changes are abnormal or different from what someone your age and lifestyle would normally have?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I mean, I know guys my age who farm, and they have lines and such. But... mine just seems worse. I don't really know what's normal anymore. I just know what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your skin changes make you look physically deformed or unrecognizable to yourself, or do you think it's more about how others might perceive it?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I don't know if \"deformed\" is the right word. I just... I don't like how it looks. And I guess I worry about what other people think when they see me. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people avoid looking at you, or act differently around you, because of how your skin looks?\n\nPatient: I couldn't say for sure. I mostly just keep to myself... out on the farm, anyway. So, I haven't really noticed anyone doing that.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly about a specific part of your skin, like a particular area on your face or hands, or does it feel more spread out and bothersome across many areas?\n\nPatient: Well... it's mostly my face and hands. I just... I can't get past how rough and worn they look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... it just bothers me. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't know. When I have to go into town, see the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself spending a lot of time each day thinking about or trying to fix or hide these skin concerns, such as checking your appearance, using cover-ups, or avoiding mirrors?\n\nPatient: I don't really use cover-ups or anything like that. But... I guess I do check a lot. Especially before I have to go into town for a loan meeting, or if my wife drags me to one of those craft fairs to help with her table. I just... I catch myself staring in the bathroom mirror, or the one in the truck. I'll keep adjusting my hat, trying to pull it down further... just trying to get it right. And I end up looking at my hands, thinking about how they look compared to the younger guys who help out during harvest. I don't know... I guess I end up wasting a fair bit of time doing that when I should be out working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about how your skin looks would still bother you even if someone reassured you that it looks normal or nothing out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I was saying, my wife already tells me it's just normal for a guy my age who works outside. But... I guess it doesn't really change how I feel about it. It just... still bothers me, no matter what she says.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin changes you’re concerned about are something that most people probably wouldn’t even notice unless you pointed it out to them?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. My wife says they wouldn't. She says I just look like a guy who's worked outdoors his whole life. But... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I just feel like when I'm in town, every banker or county person is staring right at it, even if she thinks they aren't.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly focused on how your skin looks, or does it also extend to other parts of your appearance, like your weight, hair, or overall body shape?\n\nPatient: No, it's... it's just my skin. I mean, my hair's thinning, but I'm 62, so that's... that's just how it goes. It's really just my face and my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or self-conscious about your appearance in social situations, especially when people you don’t know might see you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It... it gets in the way of things sometimes. Like, I've actually missed a few meetings with the bankers, or... just put off some farm decisions entirely. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to go sit down with people. And... well, we have this retreat and do some craft sales, and I've stopped agreeing to let them take photos of me to promote it. I just... I'd rather not be seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the skin issues you're concerned about are something that other people would clearly see and notice, or do you think most people might not even pick up on them unless you mentioned it?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said a minute ago... my wife doesn't think they notice. She tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors. But... I don't know. I just can't get past it. My face and hands just look so rough and worn. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I feel like they clearly see it. It just feels like every banker or county person is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin issues you're concerned about are things that are very minor or something that most people wouldn't even notice unless you told them?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I was just saying... my wife thinks it's minor. She says it's just normal for a man who's worked outdoors. But... no, I don't think it's minor. I just can't get past it. The cracks and the spots, the sagging... it feels pretty obvious to me. It feels like that's all those bankers or county folks are staring at.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your skin causes you a lot of distress, or makes it hard for you to function in important areas of your life, like work or family activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. Like I... I mentioned before, I've skipped some of the calls with the bankers. Just... put off making decisions for the farm because I didn't feel like I looked decent enough to face them. And, well, the photos for the retreat and the craft sales... I just don't let them take pictures of me for that anymore. It's... it's just hard.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Like I was saying, my wife already tells me it's just normal for a guy my age who works outside. But... I guess it doesn't really change how I feel about it. It just... still bothers me, no matter what she says.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin changes you’re concerned about are something that most people probably wouldn’t even notice unless you pointed it out to them?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. My wife says they wouldn't. She says I just look like a guy who's worked outdoors his whole life. But... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I just feel like when I'm in town, every banker or county person is staring right at it, even if she thinks they aren't.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly focused on how your skin looks, or does it also extend to other parts of your appearance, like your weight, hair, or overall body shape?\n\nPatient: No, it's... it's just my skin. I mean, my hair's thinning, but I'm 62, so that's... that's just how it goes. It's really just my face and my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or self-conscious about your appearance in social situations, especially when people you don’t know might see you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It... it gets in the way of things sometimes. Like, I've actually missed a few meetings with the bankers, or... just put off some farm decisions entirely. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to go sit down with people. And... well, we have this retreat and do some craft sales, and I've stopped agreeing to let them take photos of me to promote it. I just... I'd rather not be seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the skin issues you're concerned about are something that other people would clearly see and notice, or do you think most people might not even pick up on them unless you mentioned it?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said a minute ago... my wife doesn't think they notice. She tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors. But... I don't know. I just can't get past it. My face and hands just look so rough and worn. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I feel like they clearly see it. It just feels like every banker or county person is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin issues you're concerned about are things that are very minor or something that most people wouldn't even notice unless you told them?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I was just saying... my wife thinks it's minor. She says it's just normal for a man who's worked outdoors. But... no, I don't think it's minor. I just can't get past it. The cracks and the spots, the sagging... it feels pretty obvious to me. It feels like that's all those bankers or county folks are staring at.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your skin causes you a lot of distress, or makes it hard for you to function in important areas of your life, like work or family activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. Like I... I mentioned before, I've skipped some of the calls with the bankers. Just... put off making decisions for the farm because I didn't feel like I looked decent enough to face them. And, well, the photos for the retreat and the craft sales... I just don't let them take pictures of me for that anymore. It's... it's just hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry more about how your skin looks, or about what people might think or say about it if they notice it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I haven't thought about it like that. I guess... I just know it looks bad. I'm not sure which one bothers me more, to be honest.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't want to be seen like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the changes in your skin look as bad to others as they do to you, or do you think others might not notice them as much as you do?\n\nPatient: I... I couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. \n\nMy wife claims they don't notice it, but... I don't know. It's hard to imagine they don't see it when it's right there on my face. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well... my wife thought I should come talk to someone. She says I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors all his life, but... I don't know. I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. \n\nThe cracks, the sun spots, the sagging... it's just hard to ignore. Whenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or the county people, I just... I know they're staring right at it. So... I guess that's what brings me in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself spending a lot of time thinking about these skin changes, or do you feel that they interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I suppose I do think about it a fair bit. I put off some farm decisions because I couldn't get myself looking... acceptable enough to go sit down with people. And my wife... well, I stopped agreeing to let her take photos of me to promote the retreat and the craft sales we do. I just... I don't want to be seen like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the changes in your skin look as bad to others as they do to you, or do you think others might not notice them as much as you do?\n\nPatient: I... I couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. \n\nMy wife claims they don't notice it, but... I don't know. It's hard to imagine they don't see it when it's right there on my face. I just figure they have to be looking at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your skin changes are abnormal or different from what someone your age and lifestyle would normally have?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I mean, I know guys my age who farm, and they have lines and such. But... mine just seems worse. I don't really know what's normal anymore. I just know what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your skin changes make you look physically deformed or unrecognizable to yourself, or do you think it's more about how others might perceive it?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I don't know if \"deformed\" is the right word. I just... I don't like how it looks. And I guess I worry about what other people think when they see me. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people avoid looking at you, or act differently around you, because of how your skin looks?\n\nPatient: I couldn't say for sure. I mostly just keep to myself... out on the farm, anyway. So, I haven't really noticed anyone doing that.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly about a specific part of your skin, like a particular area on your face or hands, or does it feel more spread out and bothersome across many areas?\n\nPatient: Well... it's mostly my face and hands. I just... I can't get past how rough and worn they look. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... it just bothers me. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't know. When I have to go into town, see the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself spending a lot of time each day thinking about or trying to fix or hide these skin concerns, such as checking your appearance, using cover-ups, or avoiding mirrors?\n\nPatient: I don't really use cover-ups or anything like that. But... I guess I do check a lot. Especially before I have to go into town for a loan meeting, or if my wife drags me to one of those craft fairs to help with her table. I just... I catch myself staring in the bathroom mirror, or the one in the truck. I'll keep adjusting my hat, trying to pull it down further... just trying to get it right. And I end up looking at my hands, thinking about how they look compared to the younger guys who help out during harvest. I don't know... I guess I end up wasting a fair bit of time doing that when I should be out working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about how your skin looks would still bother you even if someone reassured you that it looks normal or nothing out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I was saying, my wife already tells me it's just normal for a guy my age who works outside. But... I guess it doesn't really change how I feel about it. It just... still bothers me, no matter what she says.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin changes you’re concerned about are something that most people probably wouldn’t even notice unless you pointed it out to them?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I just feel like when I'm in town, every banker or county person is staring right at it, even if she thinks they aren't.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern mainly focused on how your skin looks, or does it also extend to other parts of your appearance, like your weight, hair, or overall body shape?\n\nPatient: No, it's... it's just my skin. I mean, my hair's thinning, but I'm 62, so that's... that's just how it goes. It's really just my face and my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or self-conscious about your appearance in social situations, especially when people you don’t know might see you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It... it gets in the way of things sometimes. Like, I've actually missed a few meetings with the bankers, or... just put off some farm decisions entirely. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to go sit down with people. And... well, we have this retreat and do some craft sales, and I've stopped agreeing to let them take photos of me to promote it. I just... I'd rather not be seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the skin issues you're concerned about are something that other people would clearly see and notice, or do you think most people might not even pick up on them unless you mentioned it?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said a minute ago... my wife doesn't think they notice. It feels like that's all those bankers or county folks are staring at.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your skin causes you a lot of distress, or makes it hard for you to function in important areas of your life, like work or family activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. And... well, we have this retreat and do some craft sales, and I've stopped agreeing to let them take photos of me to promote it. I just... I'd rather not be seen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the skin issues you're concerned about are something that other people would clearly see and notice, or do you think most people might not even pick up on them unless you mentioned it?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said a minute ago... my wife doesn't think they notice. She tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors. But... I don't know. I just can't get past it. My face and hands just look so rough and worn. The cracks, the spots, the sagging... I feel like they clearly see it. It just feels like every banker or county person is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the skin issues you're concerned about are things that are very minor or something that most people wouldn't even notice unless you told them?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I was just saying... my wife thinks it's minor. She says it's just normal for a man who's worked outdoors. But... no, I don't think it's minor. I just can't get past it. The cracks and the spots, the sagging... it feels pretty obvious to me. It feels like that's all those bankers or county folks are staring at.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your skin causes you a lot of distress, or makes it hard for you to function in important areas of your life, like work or family activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. Like I... I mentioned before, I've skipped some of the calls with the bankers. Just... put off making decisions for the farm because I didn't feel like I looked decent enough to face them. And, well, the photos for the retreat and the craft sales... I just don't let them take pictures of me for that anymore. It's... it's just hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry more about how your skin looks, or about what people might think or say about it if they notice it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I haven't thought about it like that. I guess... I just know it looks bad. I'm not sure which one bothers me more, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your skin gets in the way of your ability to focus on work, conversations, or other daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah. Like I was just saying a minute ago... it does. It's hard to focus on farm decisions or... or sit through those calls with the bankers when I'm just worrying about looking acceptable. I can't really concentrate on the business side of things. And... like I mentioned with the photos for the sales... I just step away from it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts, like checking your appearance over and over or trying to mentally replay how people reacted to your skin after an interaction?\n\nPatient: I suppose I do. If I have to go to a loan meeting, or... or set up a table at the craft fair... I'll end up checking the bathroom mirror a lot. And the truck mirror. \n\nJust... turning my hat this way and that. And I'll catch myself looking at my hands, comparing them to the younger guys working the farm. I just... I end up wasting a lot of time doing it. Time I should be using to actually run the place."
